## Context

- Goal: string speed of the V8 and SpiderMonkey class [R06]. Property keys, shapes and ICs compare one word, so a key must be one cell per text.
- G6: hostile input must not degrade the engine (hash flooding, huge strings).
- Depends on: H6 (cell values), H8 (word0 kind byte and flags), H10 (CSS keeps interior pointers), H12 (no address hashing), H13 (stable atom indices). Depended on by: H17 (shape keys), H20 (the builtin read API), H35 (units name atoms by index).

## Decision

SeqLatin1, SeqUtf16, rope (length ≥ 13), slice (length ≥ 13), thin, external. Atoms with a cached hash and an array-index bit. Never a Rust `String` as the JS representation. Snapshot atoms use H13 indices (02 §6).

**v0 notes (as built, M0 week 4).**

| Item | v0 behavior | Where |
|---|---|---|

[2026-10-29, M5-P3 STR3:] the runtime makes SLICED cells through the part-2 ABI method `Cx::substring` (String.prototype.substring, slice and substr); `string::substring` was test-only from M4-P2 to M5-P3 | `crates/ep9_vm/src/kinds.rs`; `register_kinds` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/string.rs:161`) |

| Hash | SipHash-1-3 over UTF-16 code-unit values (LE), so both encodings hash alike; a cached index hashes its u32 value | `sip13_units_with` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/string.rs:1253`) |

Text from Rust: hash, probe, allocate first, then insert | `intern` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/atoms.rs:317`), `intern_text` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/atoms.rs:391`) |

| Single characters | 256 Latin-1 atoms, isolate roots, filled lazily; `s[i]` and the string iterator use them | `char_at` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/string.rs:763`) |
| Units | every unit string is interned at load (root range of the unit) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/unit.rs:170` |
| Keys | ToPropertyKey atomizes (flatten, then intern; the rope or slice forwards); no number keys: "1" is an atom with the index cached (W4-NO-NUMBER-KEYS); `number_key` reads the number-string cache first | `crates/ep9_vm/src/key.rs:60` |
| Key compare | same bits, or two atoms → unequal at once; a non-atom key takes one content compare and bumps a debug counter | `key_eq` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/key.rs:45`), `probe` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/key.rs:163`) |
| Limit | 2^29 − 24 code units (V8); `concat` returns `None` and callers throw RangeError "Invalid string length"; an error message or stack text from Rust over the limit becomes `<error>` (V8's text, H19-Q5) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/string.rs:107`; `crates/ep9_vm/src/natives.rs:1575` |
| Read API | `flatten(cx, s)` (a GC point for a CONS, which becomes (flat, "") in place; SEQ and SLICED come back as they are; M4-P1; a THIN cell gives its atom, M4-P2), then `view(&cx, s) -> JsStr<'cx>` (flat kinds, THIN included); the legacy `str_view` sites are gone (M4-P1 STR1, V9-A2) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/string.rs:616` |

- **Why a `JsStr` is safe in VM code.** The heap never moves cells (S0-S2), and the conservative scan keeps a cell alive from an interior pointer (`find_cell`, `crates/ep9_heap/src/trace.rs:931`). So a view held in a Rust local stays valid across an allocation. The borrow rule (`view` borrows `&Cx`) protects builtins, which get no `&Runtime` (H20 `abi::Cx`), and a future moving GC (S3) [I49].
- **The embedder view.** `Isolate::string_view` does not root the string, and the embedder can keep a view where the conservative scan does not look (a `Vec`). So it borrows the isolate with `&mut self`: no isolate call runs while the view lives. A shared borrow is not enough, because a `&self` call can allocate or collect: in the in-crate tests and the spike-native build, `Isolate::heap` (`Heap::collect`, `Heap::alloc`, `Heap::alloc_shape`). The raw `Isolate::global` (it interned its name) was one more until M3-P3 removed it, and the default build has no `Isolate::heap` since M3-P3 fix round 5 (H20-Q4). A raw `Value` is not a root, so `string_view` also checks it before it reads it: it reads only a live string cell of this isolate's heap (`Heap::is_cell`), and gives `None` for a freed cell, a cell of another heap or bits that do not start a cell (H20-Q4, Codex W4-1).
- **GC rule** (W4-GC-RULES). The weak source holds an `Rc` clone of the table and borrows it during a GC. Interning never holds a borrow across an allocation: it allocates first, then borrows and inserts. A held borrow makes the GC panic ("borrowed across a GC point").
- **Why only CONS and SLICED cells become THIN** (M4-P2). A THIN cell writes the atom at +16. In a CONS or SLICED cell, +16 holds a pointer, and a `JsStr` of that string reads another cell (a leaf or the parent), which the view keeps alive (conservative scan of interior pointers). In a SEQ cell, +16 holds the code units that a live `JsStr` may still read, and a SEQ cell under 24 B has no room. All three kinds are 32 B, so the heap stays parsable when the kind byte changes.
- **Why the number cache lives in Rust memory** (rule 8, M4-P2). Its strings are not roots and are never read after a GC: the weak source bumps the epoch at every collection, before the sweep, and an entry of an older epoch counts as empty. So the cache never holds a Value across a collection, and it costs O(1) per GC.

**Symbols (v0, as built, M0 week 4).** A property key is a string atom or a symbol. Both compare by bits: an atom is the one cell of its text, a symbol is its own identity.

| Item | v0 behavior | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Cell | SYMBOL (kind 8), 32 B: +8 `[[Description]]` (a string or undefined), +16 identity hash (int32, 30 bits), +24 flags (int32: bit 0 registered, bit 1 well-known, bit 2 private, reserved for M1) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:59` |
| Identity hash | the next value of a per-isolate splitmix64 PRNG (H12-Q3), never the address. Seed: 60 bits of the per-process SipHash key, read through the keyed hash of two fixed texts, so `EP9_HASH_SEED` fixes it and every isolate of one process starts the same sequence. 30 bits, the range of a string's table hash, so one shape index serves both | `SymbolState` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:249`), `prng_seed` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:292`) |
| Well-known symbols | the 13 of §6.1.5.1 plus `dispose` and `asyncDispose`; descriptions "Symbol.iterator" and so on; made by `symbol::install` in realm init; isolate roots in `WellKnownSymbol` order | `install` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:387-409`), `well_known` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:412`) |
| `%Symbol.prototype%` | an ordinary object whose prototype is `%Object.prototype%`; the prototype of Symbol wrappers; a symbol root until the builtin tables give it an intrinsic slot (P2-BTAB) | `prototype` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:418`) |
| `Symbol.for` registry | per isolate; open addressing keyed by the key's content hash (never an address); keys are atoms, so a probe compares bits; strong: a registered symbol lives as long as the isolate. `key_for` is the description of a registered symbol | `symbol_for` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:461`), `key_for` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:479`) |
| Roots | one precise root source (it holds an `Rc` clone): the well-known symbols, `%Symbol.prototype%`, the class-fields key (H19-Q7), the identity-hash key (`symbol::identity_hash_key`, H12-Q1; M3-P1), then every registry entry. `symbol_for` allocates first, then borrows the registry and inserts (W4-GC-RULES) | `install` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:387-409`) |
| Keys | ToPropertyKey gives a symbol back as it is (also when ToPrimitive returns one); `probe` classifies a symbol as Bits; `key_hash` gives its identity hash; `key_text` prints `Symbol(desc)` | `crates/ep9_vm/src/key.rs:60`, `:163`, `:176`, `:140` |
| Conversions | `typeof` "symbol"; ToBoolean true; ToNumber and ToString throw TypeError ("Cannot convert a Symbol value to a number" / "to a string", as V8); ToPrimitive calls `@@toPrimitive` with "default", "number" or "string"; IsLooselyEqual steps 11-12 take symbols | `crates/ep9_vm/src/conv.rs:100`, `:161`, `:249`, `:286`, `:345` |
| Text | SymbolDescriptiveString as Rust text for messages and `describe` (`descriptive_text`), and as a heap string (`descriptive_string`, RangeError past the length limit) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:497`, `:508` |
| Wrappers | PRIMITIVE_WRAPPER with one fixed field through the fixed-field API; ToObject wraps a string, number, boolean or symbol with the realm's prototype; a String wrapper has own `length` {w: f, e: f, c: f} and indices {w: f, e: t, c: f} | `new_wrapper` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/wrapper.rs:29`), `to_object` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/conv.rs:304`), `string_own_property` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/wrapper.rs:66`) |

- **Why a PRNG and not a counter.** A counter makes hashes of symbols made in a row adjacent, which clusters them in a power-of-two hash index. H12-Q3 proposes a per-isolate PRNG; V8 also takes symbol hashes from the isolate's random number generator. The seed follows the string hash seed, so a run with `EP9_HASH_SEED` is reproducible.
- **Why the registry is strong.** `Symbol.for(k)` must return the same symbol for the life of the realm, and a program can observe identity (`===`) even when it holds no reference in between. A weak registry would need the "symbol can be held weakly" rules of §9.13 CanBeHeldWeakly; registered symbols cannot be.

### Freeze table (README §3.4)

H19 freezes by part (plan v10 V10-A1, V10-D60): every built row at M1a, and the external kinds, which are not built, at M4 exit, so H19 never freezes an unbuilt kind. The `Atom` ids are a third part, append-only, with no freeze point (MODES-CHECK-w21 MC-w21-4). The T0 tuning value 13 is a fourth part, gated at A4 as the H17 and H18 tuning parts are, so strings core does not hold it (M5-P5-ORCH-5, MODES-CHECK-w22 MC-w22-15).

| Part | What it holds | Freeze point | After the freeze point | Check |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| strings core | Every built row of this ADR, except what the parts below hold (the value 13 in the Decision and the Kinds row is in "strings tuning (gated)"): the kinds SEQ_LATIN1, SEQ_UTF16, CONS, SLICED and THIN with their layouts (the Decision, the v0 notes Kinds and Header rows), the hash field, the hash and its seed, the weak atom table and the number-string cache, interning and atomizing, the `atoms!` list as a set of isolate roots (its ids are the "atom ids (append-only)" part), the single-character atoms, unit interning, keys and the key compare, the length limit, the read API, the symbol rows, every Rules row (the kind rule with its T0 clause: it binds the runtime `string::concat` and `string::substring`; its value 13 is in "strings tuning (gated)"; the as-built rows are facts about T0, not rules for other tiers) and the filled H19-Q1, Q4, Q5, Q6 and Q7. Not in a part: the H19-Q2 and H19-Q3 proposals (not built; their build is a fill) | M1a | Frozen-part rules (README §3.2) | the Rules checks; `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/strings.rs` and `tests/symbols.rs`; test262 with and without `--gc-stress` (Validated by) |
| external strings | The reserved kinds 6-7 (EXT_LATIN1, EXT_UTF16) behind the shared header (length at +8, hash field at +12): their layout after the header, their readers, the embedder API that makes them (runtime alpha embedder strings) and their GC rule. Owner: the strings chain. Not built: the readers panic on kinds 6-7 today | M4 exit | Frozen-part rules | the tests that land with the build (none today) |
| atom ids (append-only) | The `Atom` ids of the `atoms!` list (`crates/ep9_vm/src/atoms.rs:71`): 36 names, ids 0-35 (`Atom::id`; `Callee` is 35). A new atom takes the next id at the end; no id is renumbered, reused or removed. Units and snapshots name atoms by these ids (H19-Q3), so the ids are what an AOT unit needs to stay valid | None | An append is a fill (README §3.4 "Append-only parts"); an existing id never changes; G8 never counts an append | `tests::strings::the_atoms_list_is_append_only` (ids 0-35 by text, each id its index); `tests::layout5::callee_is_a_predefined_atom` (id 35, `Atom::ALL[35]`) |
| strings tuning (gated) | The T0 tuning value 13 of the kind rule: the shortest rope (`string::MIN_CONS_LENGTH`) and the shortest slice (`string::MIN_SLICE_LENGTH`); a shorter concat or substring is a flat copy. It is a fact about T0, not a rule for other tiers: no tier bakes it (the T0 clause, MC-w17-6; the form of H17 "layout tuning (gated)" and H18 "elements tuning (gated)", MODES-CHECK-w20 MC-w20-8). The kind rule itself stays in strings core (M5-P5-ORCH-5, MODES-CHECK-w22 MC-w22-15) | A4 gate (M8 exit) | Frozen-part rules after the gate; before it a retune is a normal change, and G8 does not count it | BENCH-SET (plan v10 §1.1); `tests::strings::concat_makes_a_cons_from_13_units_and_a_flat_string_below`, `tests::strings::cx_substring_makes_slices_from_13_units_and_copies_below` |

## Rules

| Rule | Check | From |
|---|---|---|
| `""` is 16 B, `"abc"` 24 B; length is the u32 at +8 for every string kind | `empty_string_is_16_bytes_and_abc_is_24` | M0 wk4 |
| One atom per text per isolate; each atom has ATOM and sits under its own hash | `AtomTable::check` after every GC and `atoms::verify` at isolate drop (debug builds); `atoms_verify_runs_and_finds_a_broken_table` | M0 wk4 |
| Unit strings and ToPropertyKey results are atoms; "1" has the index bit and index 1 | `one_name_from_two_units_and_a_concat_key_are_one_cell`, `index_strings_are_atoms_with_the_index_cached` | M0 wk4 |
| The table is weak: an unreferenced atom goes at the next GC, a referenced one stays | `unreferenced_dynamic_atoms_go_and_referenced_ones_stay` | M0 wk4 |
| Latin-1 and UTF-16 forms of one text hash alike | `latin1_and_utf16_forms_hash_alike` (10^4 texts) | M0 wk4 |
| The seed is per process; `EP9_HASH_SEED` fixes it; no hash in bytecode, units, snapshots or property order | `ep9_hash_seed_gives_the_same_hashes_in_two_runs`; review rule (H12) | M0 wk4 |
| Key compares on atoms are bit compares; the content-compare counter stays 0 | `EP9_ASSERT_ATOM_KEYS=1 cargo test -p ep9_vm` (debug); `the_atom_key_gate_fails_on_a_non_atom_compare` | M0 wk4 |
| An over-limit result throws RangeError, never panics | `over_limit_strings_throw_range_error` | M0 wk4 |
| A symbol is its own key: compared by bits, hashed by its identity hash, listed after the strings by [[OwnPropertyKeys]] | `a_symbol_works_as_a_key`, `own_property_keys_put_symbols_last_in_creation_order`, `symbol_keys_in_shapes_compare_by_bits_and_hash_by_identity` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/symbols.rs`) | M0 wk4 |
| Identity hashes come from the isolate PRNG, never the address; one seed gives one sequence | `two_isolates_with_one_seed_give_the_same_symbol_hashes` (two isolates, and two child processes with `EP9_HASH_SEED`) | M0 wk4 |
| Well-known and registered symbols survive every GC; `Symbol.for` gives one symbol per key text | `well_known_symbols_are_isolate_roots_in_order`, `the_registry_is_stable` (GC stress, verifier, liveness check) | M0 wk4 |
| `${s}` and `+s` throw TypeError; `@@toPrimitive` gets the spec hint | `template_literals_and_unary_plus_throw_on_a_symbol`, `at_at_to_primitive_gets_the_right_hint` | M0 wk4 |
| ToObject wraps each primitive; String wrapper `length` and indices have the spec attributes | `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/wrappers.rs` | M0 wk4 |
| A view from `view(&cx, ..)` cannot live across a `&mut` call; a view from `Isolate::string_view` cannot live across any isolate call | compile_fail doctests on `Isolate::string_view`: `collect_garbage` (E0499), views in a `Vec` across the `&self` call `gc_stats()` (E0502); rustdoc checks the codes on nightly | M0 wk4 |
| No new `str_view` call site; 0 sites outside tests by the freeze | `grep -rn 'str_view(' crates/ep9_vm/src --exclude-dir=tests` prints nothing (W4-STRVIEW; the definition, the module doc and the test callers are not sites) | M1a |
| The kind rule holds for the paths that allocate: a concat of 13 or more code units is a CONS cell (32 B) and a shorter one a flat SEQ copy; a substring of 13 or more units is a SLICED cell over the flat parent. [2026-10-28, M4-P5 fixer, Codex CX5-D1:] Two cases allocate nothing and give a string that already exists: a concat with an empty side gives the other side (`string::concat`), and a substring gives the parent itself for the full span, and this check comes first, so an empty parent gives itself too (a SEQ cell with no units can exist: `new_string_latin1` with no bytes makes one); only an empty span of a non-empty parent gives the empty atom (`string::substring`; the empty-parent order added 2026-10-28 for Codex CX5-D2). [2026-10-29, M5-P3 STR3; MODES-CHECK MC-w17-6, plan v10 V10-D44:] The kind rule binds the runtime `string::concat` and `string::substring`. 13 is a tuning value. The as-built rows are facts about T0, not rules for other tiers. `string::substring` is built code since M5-P3 (it was test-only from M4-P2): the part-2 ABI method `Cx::substring` (H20) calls it, and String.prototype.substring, slice and substr (Annex B, since the M5-P3 integration fixer) call that, so a builtin substring of 13 or more units is a SLICED cell. A slice keeps its whole parent alive while it lives, and a GC frees both after its last use. A CONS flattens in place once; ropes read, compare and hash like their flat text, with no recursion | `concat_makes_a_cons_from_13_units_and_a_flat_string_below` (it also asserts that `"" + s` and `s + ""` are `s`), `substring_makes_a_slice_from_13_units_over_the_flat_parent` (it also asserts that the full span gives the parent and an empty span of its 36-unit parent the empty atom), `flatten_writes_the_flat_form_once_into_the_cons`, `deep_ropes_read_flatten_and_compare_without_recursion`, `a_rope_hashes_and_compares_like_its_flat_text` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/strings.rs`, GC stress and the heap verifier); since M5-P3: `substring_of_an_empty_non_atom_seq_gives_the_parent` (the CX5-D2 case, also through `Cx::substring`), `cx_substring_makes_slices_from_13_units_and_copies_below`, `a_slice_keeps_its_parent_and_a_gc_frees_both_after_its_last_use` (S0 and S1, with and without GC stress, a minor GC on S1); test262 `built-ins/String/prototype/{substring,slice}` | M4-P1; the two exceptions named M4-P5; the T0 clause and `Cx::substring` M5-P3 |
| Atomizing a CONS or SLICED string gives a SEQ atom and turns the cell into a THIN forwarder (also a UTF-16 rope and a flattened rope); every reader reads it with no allocation; a THIN cell keeps its atom across GC; one atom per text under GC stress. [2026-10-27, M4-P4 fixer B, Codex H19 H-3:] Except when the text's atom is a CONS or SLICED cell that `atoms::intern` made (`key::intern_key`, the define paths): then `atomize` and `Cx::intern` give that atom, the cell stays a (flattened) CONS or a SLICED cell and no THIN cell is made (`forward` needs a SEQ atom); one atom per text still holds | `atomize_makes_a_rope_a_thin_forwarder_to_its_flat_atom`, `atomize_copies_a_slice_into_a_seq_atom_and_the_slice_forwards`, `a_utf16_rope_forwards_to_a_utf16_atom`, `intern_of_a_flattened_rope_atomizes_its_flat_part`, `ropes_and_thin_keys_under_gc_stress_keep_one_atom_per_text`; the exception: `a_rope_atom_from_intern_key_is_what_atomize_and_cx_intern_give_later` | M4-P2; the exception M4-P4 |
| ToPropertyKey of a rope is its SEQ atom (or the rope atom of the exception in the row above); the same rope as a key again allocates nothing and compares by bits | `to_property_key_of_a_rope_is_the_atom_and_the_rope_forwards`, `atomize_of_a_rope_whose_text_has_an_atom_forwards_to_that_atom` | M4-P2 |
| The number-string cache gives the text's atom (keys) or string (ToString), and a GC empties it | `number_keys_and_number_strings_share_one_cache_emptied_at_gc` | M4-P2 |
| A Rust message over the length limit is `<error>`, never a panic; a message under it is whole. [2026-10-27, M4-P4 fixer B, Codex H19 H-1:] the property-access messages of `property.rs` (`error::bounded_message`) count the length from the string lengths before they read a quoted key, so an over-limit one copies and flattens nothing. Other Rust messages that quote a value through `conv::describe` (for example "x is not a function") still copy a string value before the check: a named limit, owner the month-5 plan (VM lead role) | `a_message_over_the_length_limit_is_the_v8_error_text`; `a_keyed_read_of_undefined_with_a_rope_key_past_the_limit_copies_nothing` (a 2^29 − 34 unit rope: under 1 MiB of Rust allocations, the rope stays a CONS) | M4-P2; the length-first check M4-P4 |

## Consequences

- **Gains.** A key compare is one word for atoms, the same in T0, ICs, JIT and AOT. Strings save 8 B against the week-3 header. `s.length` is one load for every kind, ropes included. Index keys stay strings, so property.rs needs no number-key path this week.
- **Costs.** Every unit string and computed key pays one hash and one table probe (a rope or slice key pays it once, then forwards). Every GC walks the atom table (O(capacity)). A number-string cache miss costs about 60 instructions (strings-m1 `concat-rope` +1.9%). A text over the limit built from Rust (for example an error message that quotes a huge key) becomes `<error>`, as in V8 (H19-Q5).
- **Follow-ups.** Ropes and slices are built (M4-P1) and thin strings (M4-P2); the H19-Q2 lookup without insert is not built (`atoms::lookup` is test-only; month 5); external strings; P2-OBJ calls `intern_key` on define paths so stored keys are always atoms; symbol keys in `probe` and `key_hash` (done, see "Symbols"); `===` of two atoms can skip the content compare (`conv.rs`, `interp/ops.rs`).

## Validation

- Unit tests in `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/strings.rs`, also under `gc_stress: 1` with the heap verifier.
- test262 exec: 0 new failures, with and without `--gc-stress 1`.
- M1a: rope and slice tests; flatten under GC stress; the `str_view` count at 0.
- M4-P2: the thin, atomize, number-cache and message tests run on a GC-stress isolate with the heap verifier (`stressed()` in `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/strings.rs`); the atom-key gate (`EP9_ASSERT_ATOM_KEYS=1`) on a test262 subset; full test262 (exec, eager, GC stress) on x86-64.

## String speed record (M4-P2, V9-D17)

A value, not a gate. The workload is `tools/bench/workloads/strings-m1.js`: 7 named micros, each checked against node v24.22.1; `var ONLY = "<name>";` runs one. Instructions: `ep9-bench`, mac a64 (Apple M5 Max), `EP9_HASH_SEED=1`, 5 runs + 1 warm-up, median, whole process (ep9 start-up is 22.6M). Base = 12cfc61 (the M4-P1 head), head = the M4-P2 STR2 branch. Net ms = median of 7 wall-clock runs minus the engine's empty-script median, on a loaded host (load average 13-29): an estimate for the "V8/SM class" question, not a gate number.

| Micro | What it measures | Base instr. | Head instr. | Change | ep9 net ms | node --jitless net ms | QuickJS net ms |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| concat-rope | `s += "abcdefgh" + i` (200k), then charCodeAt | 635,867,565 | 648,104,110 | +1.92% | 22.8 | 23.4 | 18.6 |
| index-chars | `s[i]`, charCodeAt over a flat string | 476,812,178 | 476,585,905 | −0.05% | 14.6 | 9.2 | 8.7 |
| rope-keys | `o[rope]` read, read, write per key | 445,178,988 | 383,944,418 | −13.76% | 13.1 | 13.3 | 12.2 |
| number-keys | `o[i * 3]` on a plain object | 697,234,147 | 541,824,962 | −22.29% | 18.1 | 6.0 | 3.1 |
| number-strings | `"" + n`, `String(x.5)` | 870,820,214 | 778,463,663 | −10.61% | 23.9 | 17.6 | 23.0 |
| compare | `<` and `===` on run-time strings | 145,240,161 | 129,350,109 | −10.94% | 4.0 | 7.0 | 2.5 |
| substrings | substring, indexOf, slice (copies) | 213,614,437 | 213,366,551 | −0.12% | 6.5 | 5.1 | 2.2 |
| all | the 7 in one process | 3,361,967,688 | 3,044,121,252 | −9.45% | 100.0 | 55.4 | 68.5 |

- Reading (net ms ratios, estimates): ep9 is within about 1.4x of node --jitless and QuickJS on `concat-rope` (0.97x, 1.23x), `rope-keys` (0.99x, 1.08x) and `number-strings` (1.36x, 1.04x); on `compare` it is 0.57x node --jitless and 1.6x QuickJS. It is behind on `number-keys` (3.0x, 5.8x: the keyed path for an int key on a plain object, not the string code), `index-chars` (1.6x, 1.7x) and `substrings` (1.3x, 3.0x: substrings copy until a `Cx::substring` caller makes SLICED cells). These are inputs of the M1a speed check (Reopens if, row 1).
- BENCH-SET at the same pair (instructions, median): raytrace −20.0%, earley-boyer −0.68%, deltablue −0.32%; t0-loop, t0-branchy, t0-call, t0-prop, ci-v0, richards, crypto, splay and navier-stokes within ±0.1%. The concat probe (`s += "abcdefgh" + i`, 200k, then 50 substrings) takes 0.03 s.

## Open questions

| ID | Question | Needed by | Proposed default |
|---|---|---|---|
| H19-Q1 | When does a rope flatten (index, compare, atomize)? | M1 | V8: flatten on index and on atomize. **Filled 2026-10-27 (as built, M4-P1 STR1 and M4-P2 STR2; V9-D13):** a CONS flattens in place, once: it becomes (flat, ""), so a second flatten allocates nothing. Flatten runs on index (`char_at`, `Cx::flatten` before a builtin reads units), on atomize (`atoms::atomize`, used by ToPropertyKey) and before every `view`. Compare, equality, hash and `array_index` never flatten: they walk the rope and allocate nothing on the JS heap. Atomize flattens first and does not probe with a rope walk: on `strings-m1` `rope-keys` the walk was 26% slower than flatten + flat hash (M4-P2 measure) |
| H19-Q2 | Atom lookup without insert for [[Get]] | M1 | Probe only; a miss means "no such key". **Filled 2026-10-27 (Draft proposal, M4-P2 STR2; not built):** a [[Get]], [[HasProperty]] or `in` with a string key that is not an atom probes the table and never inserts. A miss means no object has the key (stored keys are always atoms, `intern_key`), so the lookup ends as "absent" and makes no atom; a hit gives the atom (and a CONS or SLICED key becomes THIN). The probe exists as `atoms::lookup` (test builds). The caller is the keyed slow path (`interp/slow`, `property.rs`), which no string role owns: the keyed role (M4-P3) or the month-5 plan builds it. **Note 2026-10-27 (M4-P4 fixer B, Codex H19 H-2):** a miss shows only that no stored key has the text. Array elements (`object::array_set` stores no key), String wrapper indices (`wrapper::string_own_property` makes them) and the routed kinds (arguments, module namespace, proxy: their hooks answer) have properties with no atom, also as prototypes. So the shortcut may end a lookup as absent only when the key is not an array index (`key::array_index_of`) or no object on the chain can hold an index without a key (no array, no String wrapper, no routed kind; `shape::proto_index_keys` is false for the chain), and never past a routed kind on the chain. Owner: the month-5 plan row that builds it (VM lead role). Its regression cases: an index string built at run time (not an atom) read from an array, from a String wrapper and from an ordinary object whose prototype is an array, a String wrapper or a proxy; each finds the property (`[[Get]]`, `[[HasProperty]]` and `in`) |
| H19-Q3 | How units and snapshots name atoms by index (H13) | M1 (lists), A1 | `Atom` ids first, then a unit atom table. **Filled 2026-10-27 (Draft proposal, M4-P2 STR2):** index 0-35 are the `atoms!` ids (0-34 when this was filled; M5-P0 added `Callee`, 35) (`Atom::id`, append-only, `crates/ep9_vm/src/atoms.rs:71`); a unit names its other strings by its own string-table index, and `unit.rs` interns them at load (they are roots of the unit). A snapshot (A1) writes the same two ranges. Nothing changes in month 4 |
| H19-Q4 | Thin strings: when can a string not become an atom in place? | M1 | Ropes and external strings get a thin forwarder. **Filled 2026-10-27 (as built, M4-P2 STR2):** a SEQ string becomes the atom in place (ATOM flag) when its text has no atom, and stays as it is when one exists. Through `atomize`, a CONS or SLICED string never becomes an atom: a CONS is flattened and its flat part becomes (or finds) the atom; a SLICED text with no atom is copied into a SEQ atom, so no atom keeps a large parent alive. The CONS or SLICED cell then becomes THIN (kind 5, +16 the SEQ atom): the next atomize is one load and the old parts or the parent can die. A SEQ cell never becomes THIN (see "Why only CONS and SLICED cells become THIN"). `atoms::intern` (no GC point; the define paths) keeps the old rule for a CONS or SLICED string whose text has no atom: the cell becomes the atom. [2026-10-27, M4-P4 fixer B, Codex H19 H-3:] So a CONS or SLICED atom can exist; `atomize` and `Cx::intern` of an equal string then give that atom unchanged, and the string does not become THIN (`forward` needs a SEQ atom): no one-load forward for it (test `a_rope_atom_from_intern_key_is_what_atomize_and_cx_intern_give_later`). External strings: not built |
| H19-Q5 | Over-limit text built from Rust (messages) | M1 | Truncate quoted values in messages; RangeError elsewhere. **Filled 2026-10-27 (as built, M4-P2 STR2), not the proposed default:** quoted values stay whole, because node v24.22.1 keeps them (a 5,000-unit key gives a 5,048-unit message). A message or stack text built in Rust that is over the length limit becomes `<error>`, the text node v24.22.1 gives for `undefined[s]` with `s` of 2^29 − 24 units (`error::message_string`). RangeError stays for every string that JS code builds. [2026-10-27, M4-P4 fixer B, Codex H19 H-1:] the property-access messages count the length first (`error::bounded_message`), so `undefined[k]` with a rope `k` near the limit gives `<error>` with no flatten and no Rust copy of `k` (see the Rules row) |
| H19-Q6 | A number-to-string cache for `o[i]` on non-arrays | M1 | V8-style small cache keyed by the number. **Filled 2026-10-27 (as built, M4-P2 STR2):** 1,024 direct-mapped entries per isolate next to the atom table (24 KiB), keyed by the number's bits (an int32 picks the slot by its value). `conv::number_string` (ToString) and `key::number_key` read it; a key hit is the atom (a cached plain string is interned in place first). A GC empties it in O(1) (epoch bump). 64 entries thrashed on a loop over 500 keys (+2.7% on `number-keys`); with 1,024: `number-keys` −22%, raytrace −20% (BENCH-SET); a miss costs about 60 instructions (`concat-rope` +1.9%) |
| H19-Q7 | Private names (`#x`): a SYMBOL with the private flag (V8) or a kind of their own | M1 (classes) | **Filled 2026-10-22 (as built, M0 month 2 P2): a SYMBOL with the private flag, bit 2** (V8). `CreatePrivateName(description)` (runtime id 23) makes a new one per evaluation of a class (`symbol::new_private`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:336`). A private element is an own entry of the object's storage keyed by that symbol (attributes WRITABLE); a class with private methods or accessors adds one private-name brand per instance (or to the constructor for static ones). `[[OwnPropertyKeys]]` and for-in never list a private key (`crates/ep9_vm/src/property.rs:1906`); the keyed opcodes send it to `interp/slow/private.rs` (own lookup only, no IC, C7). An add on a non-extensible object throws a TypeError (M2-PRIVATE-EXT). One more private symbol per isolate, the class-fields key (`class_fields_key`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/symbol.rs:445`), holds a class's instance-field initializer on its constructor (C8). Since M3-P1 a second one, the identity-hash key (`identity_hash_key`, made by `symbol::install` after the class-fields key), holds an object's identity hash (H12-Q1); both are one per isolate, shared by every realm |

## Reopens if

- The M1a string speed check (02 §6 H19) misses the V8/SM class on the string workloads.
- The atom-table check fails under GC stress, or the content-compare counter cannot reach 0 once P2-OBJ interns stored keys.

CONS, SLICED and THIN are `Fixed(32)` and share `trace_rope` (+16, +24), the SEQ kinds trace nothing (`register_kinds`); `MIN_CONS_LENGTH` 13 and `MIN_SLICE_LENGTH` 13 (test builds: `substring` is test-only, so no SLICED cell is made outside tests); the hash field: bit 0 computed, bit 1 an index below 2^24 (`MAX_CACHED_INDEX`), else 30 hash bits (`HASH_MASK`); `MAX_STRING_LENGTH` 2^29 - 24; `flatten` turns a CONS into (flat, "") once, with two barriered stores, and gives a THIN cell's atom; `atomize` flattens a CONS first, copies a SLICED text with no atom into a SEQ atom, and `forward` makes the cell THIN only when the atom is a SEQ string; ToPropertyKey (`key.rs`) and `Cx::intern` (`abi/mod.rs`) call `atomize`, `key::intern_key` (the define paths) calls `atoms::intern`; the number cache has 1,024 entries (24 KiB) and its epoch moves in the atom weak source, which runs at every collection, minor ones included (the heap's collect path calls `roots.visit_weak` for both); JS text is never a Rust `String` (string cells only; `to_rust_string` copies for messages and tests). **Rules:** the 26 test names of the Rules table exist (18 in `tests/strings.rs`, 8 in `tests/symbols.rs`), and so do the 2 `compile_fail` doctests on `Isolate::string_view` (E0499 across `collect_garbage`, E0502 across `gc_stats`); Verdict **needs-attention**, a static review (no test run). [high] H-1: the Rules row "A Rust message over the length limit" and H19-Q5 claim a bound, but `undefined[hugeRope]` formats the key through `describe` and `string::to_rust_string` (the keyed read of `get_keyed` in property.rs at 2e016d0) before `error::message_string` checks the limit, so a rope near the limit costs hundreds of MB of Rust text before `<error>`; the named test checks `bounded_text` on a built string only. [medium] H-2: the H19-Q2 proposal ends a lookup as "absent" on an atom miss, but array elements and String wrapper indices (and inherited ones) have no atom: it needs index, exotic and prototype-chain guards, an owner and regression cases. [medium] H-3: the Rules row on atomizing and H19-Q4 promise a SEQ atom and a THIN forwarder, but `atoms::intern` can install a CONS or SLICED atom and `atomize` then returns it unchanged (no THIN cell); the cited tests cover absent or SEQ atoms only. No material H21 finding. Sort: all three (a) (H19 is Accepted at M4-P5, so its text must be true). Handled by fixer B: H-1 code and test edae2ba plus the dated notes of the 2026-10-27 fixer B fill row; H-2 the Q2 note and H-3 the Rules, Interning and Q4 notes (the fixer B rewrite row), H-3's test in edae2ba. The row and its Decision notes (29d29a7: the Rules row 'A Rust message over the length limit' and H19-Q5) against edae2ba: `error::bounded_message` sums `Part::Text` lengths and `key_units` (a string's `str_len`; a symbol's 'Symbol()' plus its description's length; else `describe`) and returns `<error>` past `MAX_STRING_LENGTH` before `key_text` runs. The read, set, read-only, getter-only, add, create, redefine, define, delete and proxy-define messages of property.rs use it; at 2e016d0 the keyed forms used `describe(k).trim_matches('"')`, which cut quote characters at a key's ends; now a string key is its whole text, as Q5 says. `undefined[k]` with a rope near the limit gives `<error>` with no flatten. Every older Rules test keeps its result (`tests::strings` 46 passed, 45 in `--release`, with `a_message_over_the_length_limit_is_the_v8_error_text`). The named limit is true: `has` (the 'in' message) and the instanceof 'is not a function' text still format `describe(...)` first. Author evidence (row 4): the mutation (4,294,973,455 B against a 1 MiB bound), the CLI peak RSS 1.09 GB against 2.16 GB at dba9e30, 17 of 21 messages equal to node v24.22.1 and the 4 others as at dba9e30. The row's 'about 1 GiB of Rust copies' fits the peak RSS difference (about 1.07 GB); the mutation counts 4.29 GB allocated in all. Plan: H-1 came from the orchestrator's Codex slice on H19 + H21 (v9 §6.7), and H19 must be true for the M4-P5 Accept. Kind fill (H19 is Draft): agree: dated notes add the length-first rule; no Rules check, test or golden changes its result. (1) H-3: the Interning row, the atomizing Rules row (with ToPropertyKey's row) and H19-Q4 now say that a CONS or SLICED atom made by `atoms::intern` (`key::intern_key`, the define paths) can exist and that `atomize` and `Cx::intern` then give it with no THIN cell. Code: `intern` of a rope not yet flattened whose text has no atom calls `string::set_atom` on the rope; `atomize` of an equal CONS flattens it, then `intern` finds the old atom through its flat part; `forward` makes THIN only when the atom `is_seq`; `Cx::intern` calls `atomize`. The test `a_rope_atom_from_intern_key_is_what_atomize_and_cx_intern_give_later` asserts exactly this (one atom per text, the rope stays a CONS, a property set under the rope atom is found through another equal rope) and passes (debug and `--release`). (2) H-2: the Q2 note on the unbuilt proposal: `object::array_set` stores no key, `wrapper::string_own_property` makes wrapper indices, `shape::proto_index_keys` is the isolate's flag for index-holding prototypes; the guards and the regression cases follow from that. The `str` at 1095 and 1104 are ELEMENTS (Value) stores, and no other file in `spikes/` or `crates/` writes `ELEMENTS_DOUBLE_DATA` (grep). `PURE_NAN_BITS` (`value.rs:39`) equals `CANONICAL_NAN` (`object.rs:325`; const assert at `object.rs:1029`) and is not the hole (`object.rs:323`). So the NaN rule holds and only the record was wrong: sort (b) (M5-P4-CODEX-R4) is right.
